This proof can be viewed as an instance of a more general phenomenon. In this proof, the
R_{n}
are the restriction to
p_{n}(\mathbb{M})
of the
characteristic sequence
of
\varphi(x,y)
, defined by Malliaris:

Definition 3.4
.

(
[
30
]

Let
\varphi(x,y)
be a formula. The
characteristic sequence
of
\varphi(x,y)
is the sequence of hypergraphs, on the vertices
\mathbb{M}^{|y|}
, defined by

R_{n}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=:(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\models\exists x\wedge_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(x,y_{i})

On the other hand, within
\mathcal{M}_{p}
, the
R_{n}
are just a sequence of hypergraphs, and do not describe a pattern of consistency internally to
\mathcal{M}_{p}
. Nonetheless, by showing that a particular configuration, the
compatible order property
, arises among the
R_{n}
, we get a description of the complexity of
\varphi(x,y)

in the original theory
T
. In
[
30
]
, Malliaris introduces some hypergraph configurations corresponding, via the characteristic sequence, to consistency patterns (in the sense of
[
15
]
) in a first-order formula. We introduce some additional defintions to cover the case of the tree property,
\omega
-
\mathrm{DCTP}_{2}
, and
\mathrm{SOP}_{1}
; the first of these comes from Observation 5.20 of
[
30
]
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
R_{\infty}=(V,\{R_{n}\}_{n<\omega})
be a sequence of hypergraphs on a common set of vertices
V
, where
R_{n}
is an
n
-ary edge relation Then
R_{\infty}
has

(1) An
(\omega,\omega,1)
-array if there is an array
\{b_{ij}\}_{i,j\in\omega}
so that there is some fixed
k
so that, for all
i
,
\{b_{ij}\}_{j\in\omega}
is a clique, but for any
\sigma\in\omega^{\omega}
,
\{b_{i\sigma(i)}\}_{i\in\omega}
is a
k
-anticlique. (Definition 3.4.2,
[
30
]
. By Claim 3.8,
[
30
]
,
\mathrm{TP}_{2}
is equivalence to the presence of an
(\omega,\omega,1)
-array in the characteristic sequence of a formula.

(2) The
compatible order property
if there are
c_{0},\ldots,c_{n},\ldots,d_{0},\ldots,d_{n},\ldots
so that for
m<n
,
d_{0},\ldots,d_{m},c_{m+1},\ldots,c_{n}
form a clique, while for
m<n
,
\neg R_{2}(c_{m},d_{n})
. (Definition 3.10,
[
30
]
. In Conclusion 6.15 of
[
30
]
,
\mathrm{SOP}_{3}
is shown to be equivalent to the compatible order property in the characteristic sequence of a formula.)

(3)
\mathrm{MTP}
if there is some fixed
k
and parameters
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\omega^{<\omega}}
so that for each path
\sigma\in\omega^{\omega}
,
\{b_{\sigma|_{n}}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a clique, but for each node
\eta\in\omega^{<\omega}
,
\{b_{\eta\smallfrown\langle n\rangle}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
k
-anticlique. (In Observation 5.10 of
[
30
]
, the failure of a formula to be simple is observed to be equivalent to
\mathrm{MTP}
in the characteristic sequence.

(4)
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
if there are parameters
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{<\omega}}
so that
\{b_{\sigma\upharpoonleft n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a clique for any
\sigma\in 2^{\omega}
, but for any
\eta_{2}\unrhd\eta_{1}\smallfrown\langle 0\rangle
,
\{b_{\eta_{2}},b_{\eta_{1}\smallfrown\langle 1\rangle}\}
is a
2
-anticlique.

(5)
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{2}
if for some fixed
k
, there are parameters
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{<\omega}}
so that
\{b_{\sigma\upharpoonleft n}\}_{n\in\omega}
is a
k
-anticlique for any
\sigma\in 2^{\omega}
, but for any descending comb
\eta_{1}\ldots,\eta_{l}\in 2^{<\omega}
,
\{x,b_{\eta_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{l}
is a clique.

Remark 3.10
.

The letter
\mathrm{M}
in
\mathrm{MTP}
,
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
and
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{2}
stands for Malliaris.

Note that these properties are all
graph-theoretic
in the sense of Malliaris,
[
30
]
, referring only to incidence patterns of the edges, rather than their consistency. They are similar in this sense to stability or
\mathrm{NIP}
, which make no reference to consistency but only ask for graph-theoretic configurations. In
[
38
]
, Shelah shows the following:

Fact 3.11
.

Let
R(x,y)
be an unstable formula, and assume that all Boolean combinations of instances of
R(x,y)
are
\mathrm{NSOP}
. Then
R(x,y)
has the independence property.

Note that the form of this result is as follows: if a graph has one graph-theoretic configuration (instability), and an ambient model-theoretic tameness property (
\mathrm{NSOP}
, indeed quantifier-free
\mathrm{NSOP}
), then it has a more complicated graph-theoretic configuration (the independence property). It was Malliaris who first implicitly asked, in the context of strengthenings of the compatible order property (Remark 7.12,
[
31
]
), whether ambient classification-theoretic properties imply further graph-theoretic complexity gaps for hypergraphs. In the remainder of this section, we observe that model-theoretic tameness properties of hypergraph sequences that refer to consistency, such as simplicity and
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, provide additional information about their graph-theoretic structure, just as Shelah shows a gap between simplicity and independence in
\mathrm{NSOP}
graphs. We then further observe that the connection between internal properties of types and external properties of theories, including the aforementioned work of Chernikov on co-simplicity (
[
8
]
), can be reinterpreted in terms of these graph-theoretic complexity gaps for model-theoretically tame hypergraphs.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
R_{\infty}=(V,\{R_{n}\}_{n<\omega})
be sequence of hypergraphs on a common set of vertices
V
, where
R_{n}
is an
n
-ary edge relation.

(1) If
R_{\infty}
is simple (in the hypergraph language) and has
\mathrm{MTP}
, it has an
(\omega,\omega,1)
array.

(2) If
R_{\infty}
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
and has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
, then it has
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{2}
and the compatible order property.

In fact, for (1), it suffices that no quantifier-free formula has the tree property, and similarly for (2) and
\mathrm{SOP}_{1}
.

Example 3.13
.

If
R_{\infty}
is the model companion of the empty theory in the language of hypergraph sequences (or, say, the theory axiomatizing the basic properties of characteristic sequences; see
[
30
]
, Observation 2.4), then it is a simple structure. But it has
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{2}
and the compatible order property.

Proof.

(Sketch)
The argument for (1) is extracted from Chernikov’s proof in
[
8
]
that simple types are co-simple. In particular, we notice that the proof Lemma 6.13 of
[
8
]
works when the rows are general indiscnernible sequences, not just Morley sequences, and relies only on the internal simplicity of a type, not the full definition of a simple type. Suppose
R_{\infty}
has
\mathrm{MTP}
, but is simple as a structure in the language of hypergraph sequences. By the proof, which can be found in a standard reference on simplicity theory such as
[
22
]
, that formulas with the tree property fail Kim’s lemma for dividing, there is a model
M
of the theory of
R_{\infty}
, some element
b
of the monster model, and some indiscernible
k
-anticlique
I=\{b_{i}\}_{i\in I}
starting with
b_{0}=b
, as well as a Morley sequence
J=\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in\omega}
starting with
b^{\prime}_{0}=b
and forming a clique. Now suppose, by induction, that for
i\leq n
there are are
I^{i}=\{b_{j}^{i}\}_{j<\omega}
with
I^{i}\equiv_{M}I
and
b_{0}^{i}=b^{\prime}_{i}
(so the
I^{i}
are anticliques), so that for
\sigma\in\omega^{n}
,
\{b^{i}_{\sigma(i)}\}_{i\leq n}\smallfrown\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\geq n+1}
is a clique, and so that
I_{\leq n}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}b^{\prime}_{\geq n+1}
. By properties of independence in simple theories,
I_{\leq n}b_{>n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}b^{\prime}_{n+1}
. By the chain condition, take
I^{n+1}=\{b_{j}^{n+1}\}_{j<\omega}
,
I^{n+1}\equiv_{M}I
, with
b^{n+1}_{0}=b^{\prime}_{n+1}
so that
I_{n+1}
is indiscnernible over
I_{\leq n}b^{\prime}_{>n+1}
, and with
I_{\leq n}b^{\prime}_{>n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}I_{n+1}
. This suffices for the induction. Now the existence of an
(\omega,\omega,1)
-array follows.

For (2), suppose
R_{\infty}
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
in the hypergraph language, but has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
. Then as in the proof of Theorem
3.7
, there is a model
\mathcal{M}
of the theory of
R_{\infty}
and there are
M
-invariant Morley sequences
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
in the
M
-invariant type
p_{0}
and
\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
in the
M
-invariant type
p_{1}
, so that
b_{0}=b^{\prime}_{0}
,
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
is a clique, and
\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
is a
2
-anticlique. To show
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{1}
, it suffices to find a tree
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{<\omega}}
so that the paths, read downward, are Morley sequences in
p_{1}
, and the descending combs are Morley sequences in
p_{0}
. Formally, the construction will follow
[
35
]
, Lemma 4.5. Say that a tree
\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
is a
generic tree
if for
\eta\in 2^{<n}

c_{\unrhd\eta\smallfrown\langle 0\rangle}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}c_{\unrhd\eta\smallfrown\langle 1\rangle}
(two subtrees at a node are Kim-independent), and
c_{\eta}\models p_{1}(x)|_{Mc_{\rhd\eta}}
(each node satisfies the restriction of
p_{1}(x)
to its subtrees.) We prove the following claim (corresponding to Claim 4.6 of
[
35
]
):

Claim 3.14
.

Let
\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
be a generic tree, and
A
any set. Then there is some
\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}\equiv_{M}\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
with
c^{\prime}_{\eta}\models p_{0}(x)|_{MA}
for each
\eta\in 2^{\leq n}
, and with
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
n
, we may assume this is true for
\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 0\rangle}
and
\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 1\rangle}
. Namely, fin
\{c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 0\rangle}\equiv_{M}\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 0\rangle}
with
c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\models p_{0}(x)|_{MA}
for each
\langle 0\rangle\unlhd\eta\in 2^{\leq n}
and
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}\{c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 0\rangle}
, and similarly,
\{c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 1\rangle}\equiv_{M}\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 1\rangle}
with
c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\models p_{1}(x)|_{MA}
for each
\langle 1\rangle\unlhd\eta\in 2^{\leq n}
and
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}\{c^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\unrhd\langle 1\rangle}
. Recall that
c_{\unrhd\langle 0\rangle}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}c_{\unrhd\langle 1\rangle}
as
\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
is a generic tree, so by the independence theorem and an automorphism, we can find
\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\rhd\langle\rangle}\equiv_{M}\{c_{\eta}\}_{\eta\rhd\langle\rangle}
with
c^{\prime}_{\eta}\models p_{0}(x)|_{MA}
for each
\langle\rangle\lhd\eta\in 2^{\leq n}
and
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\rhd\langle\rangle}
. Finally, by the independence theorem and an automorphism, find
c^{\prime}_{\langle\rangle}\models p_{0}(x)|_{MA}\cup p_{1}(x)|_{M\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\rhd\langle\rangle}}
so that
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}\{c^{\prime}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
, as desired.
∎

By induction, we construct a
generic
tree
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{<\omega}}
so that the paths, read downward, are Morley sequences in
p_{1}
, and the descending combs are Morley sequences in
p_{0}
. Suppose we have constructed
I=\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
with these properties. By Claim
3.14
, we can find
I^{1}\equiv_{M}I\equiv_{M}I^{2}
with
I^{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}I^{2}
and for
I^{1}=\{b^{1}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
,
I^{2}=\{b^{2}_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n}}
,
b^{2}_{\eta}\models p_{0}(x)|_{MI_{1}}
for
\eta\in 2^{\leq n}
. The trees
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
of height
n
will be the subtrees of our new generic tree of height
n+1
. Finally, let
b_{*}\models q_{1}(x)|_{MI_{1}I_{2}}
be the new root. Reindexing accordingly, we get a generic tree
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in 2^{\leq n+1}}
so that the paths, read downward, are Morley sequences in
p_{1}
, and the descending combs are Morley sequences in
p_{0}
. This completes the induction.

Now the compatible order property comes from the proof of Theorem
3.7
.

∎

We connect this to the internal properties of types. We recall the defintion of co-simplicity from
[
8
]
:

Definition 3.6
.

A type
p(x)
over
A
is co-simple if there is no formula
\varphi(x,y)
,
k\geq 2
and parameters
\{b_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\omega^{<\omega}}
,
b_{\eta}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M})
so that for each path
\sigma\in\omega^{\omega}
,
\{\varphi(x,b_{\sigma|_{n}})\}_{n\in\omega}
is consistent, but for each node
\eta\in\omega^{<\omega}
,
\{\varphi(x,b_{\eta\smallfrown\langle n\rangle})\}_{n\in\omega}
is
k
-inconsistent.

Corollary 3.14.1
.

(1) (
[
8
]
, Theorem 6.17) In a
\mathrm{NTP}_{2}
theory, internally simple types are co-simple.

(2) In an
\mathrm{N}
-
\omega
-
\mathrm{DCTP}_{2}
theory or an
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theory, internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types are co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}

Proof.

(1). If
p(x)
is not co-simple then the restriction
R_{\infty}
of some charcateristic sequence to
p(\mathbb{M})
has
\mathrm{MTP}
. If
p(x)
is internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, then
R_{\infty}
is
simple
, so by the previous proposition it has an
(\infty,\infty,1)
array. So
T
must have
\mathrm{TP}_{2}
.

(2). If
p(x)
is not co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
then the restriction
R_{\infty}
of some charcateristic sequence to
p(\mathbb{M})
has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
. If
p(x)
is internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, then
R_{\infty}
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, so by the previous proposition it has
\omega
-
\mathrm{MDCTP}_{2}
and the compatible order property. So
T
must have
\omega
-
\mathrm{DCTP}_{2}
and
\mathrm{SOP}_{3}
.
∎

In other words, the fact that internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types are co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
in
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theories can be interpreted as saying that in an
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theory, the graph-theoretic complexity of a characteristic sequence must be reflected in its model-theoretic complexity in the hypergraph language.

Remark 3.15
.

For
R_{\infty}
a hypergraph sequence, define
R^{(m)}_{\infty}
to be the hypergraph sequence whose vertices are
m
-tuples of vertices of
R_{\infty}
, and define
R_{n}^{(m)}((a^{1}_{1},\ldots a^{m}_{1}),\ldots(a^{1}_{n},\ldots a^{m}_{m}))=:R_{mn}(a^{1}_{1},\ldots a^{m}_{1},\ldots a^{1}_{n},\ldots a^{m}_{m})
. For example, if
R_{\infty}
is the characteristic sequence of
\varphi(x,y)
, then
R^{(m)}_{\infty}
is the characteristic sequence of
\wedge_{i=1}^{m}\varphi(x,y_{i})
. If we consider hypergraphs up to the concatenation operation
R_{\infty}\mapsto R^{(m)}_{\infty}
, then we obtain additional information. For example, we can define
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}=\mathrm{MTP}_{1}
to mean that there exists a binary (or infinitely branching; see
[
1
]
, recounted in Fact 4.2 of
[
10
]
) tree whose paths are cliques and whose incomparable pairs are
2
-anticliques. It follows from the proof of
[
38
]
, III.7.7, III.7.11 that up to concatenation, a hypergraph sequence with
\mathrm{MTP}
either has an
(\omega,\omega,1)
array or has
\mathrm{MTP}_{1}
. So by Proposition
3.12
.2, if
R_{\infty}
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, and has
\mathrm{MTP}
, then up to concatenation it either has
\mathrm{MTP}
or an
(\omega,\omega,1)
array.

It is also worth noting that if we define
\mathrm{MATP}
to be the existence of a tree so that the antichains are cliques and the paths are
k
-anticliques, it follows from the proof of Theorem 4.8 of
[
3
]
that up to concatenation, an
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
hypergraph sequence has either
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}
or
\mathrm{MATP}
.

Although it is shown in
[
35
]
that
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
coincides with
\mathrm{NSOP}_{2}
for theories, we get additional graph-theoretic information when we assume only the quantifier-free formulas of
R_{\infty}
to be
\mathrm{NSOP}_{2}
. Namely, if
R_{\infty}
has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
, it has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}
up to concatenation. This follows from the arguments in
[
35
]
; we give an overview. If
R_{\infty}
has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{1}
, there is a model
M
and two
M
-finitely satisfiable Morley sequences, one of which is a clique and one of which is a
2
-anticlique. (That
k
may chosen to be
2
comes from
[
29
]
,
[
19
]
). Now Lemma 4.5 of
[
35
]
says that for any coheir
p(x)
over
M
and canonical coheir
q(x)
over
M
, there is a tree whose paths are Morley sequences in
p(x)
and whose descending combs are Morley sequences in
q(x)
. So if Morley sequences in
q(x)
are anticliques and Morley sequences in
p(x)
are cliques, the descending combs will be anticliques and the paths will be cliques. By the proof of Lemma 2.8 of
[
35
]
(
\mathrm{SOP}_{2}
= k-
\mathrm{DCTP}_{1}
), such a tree, up to concatenation, gives an instance of
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}
.

So because there exists a finitely satisfiable Morley sequence that is a clique, either
R_{\infty}
has
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}
and we are done, or there is also a canonical Morley sequence that is a clique. At this point, now that we have an
M
-finitely satisfiable Morley sequence that is a
2
-anticlique and a canonical Morley sequence that is a clique, we can prove Kim’s lemma for canonical Morley sequences, symmetry for Conant-independence, and the weak independence theorem for Conant-independence, all in the quantifier-free context as for
\mathrm{NSOP}_{2}
theories, and then use the technique of Conant (
[
11
]
) developed in section 6 of
[
35
]
to show the compatible order property. But up to concatenation, the compatible order property implies
\mathrm{MSOP}_{2}
(
[
30
]
, Observation 3.11).

There are
\mathrm{SOP}_{1}
formulas
\varphi(x,y)
so that
\wedge_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(x,y_{i})
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{2}
; see
[
3
]
, §6. Byunghan Kim asked, at the 2023 BIRS meeting on neostability theory, whether it can be shown that if a formula is
\mathrm{SOP}_{1}
, a related formula is
\mathrm{SOP}_{2}
. It follows from the above discussion that if a formula is
\mathrm{SOP}_{1}
,
\mathrm{SOP}_{2}
must appear in the quantifier-free formulas of its characteristic sequence.

4.
Independence of internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types in
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theories

In this section, we prove an extension of the independence theorem of Kaplan and Ramsey (
[
19
]
) to internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types in
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theories. We will use Theorem
3.7
, namely that internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types in
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theories are co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
.

While the theorem does not give
a_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}a_{2}a_{2}
,
a^{\prime\prime}_{1}
can be chosen so that any two of
a^{\prime\prime}_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
is Conant-independent from the third, somewhat similarly to Theorem 2.13 of
[
27
]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
T
be
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
, and let
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
be internally
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types over
M
. Let
a_{1}\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}_{1}\subset p_{1}(\mathbb{M})
,
a_{2}\subset p_{2}(\mathbb{M})
,
a_{3}\subset p_{3}(\mathbb{M})
. If
a_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{2}
,
a^{\prime}_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{3}
,
a_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{3}
, there is some
a^{\prime\prime}_{1}
with
a^{\prime\prime}_{1}\models\mathrm{tp}(a_{1}/Ma_{2})\cup\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}_{1}/Ma_{3})
. Moreover,
a^{\prime\prime}_{1}
can be chosen with
a_{2}a_{3}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a^{\prime\prime}_{1}
,
a_{2}a^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{3}
and
a_{3}a^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{2}
.

It is of interest that the conclusion does not hold for
\mathrm{NSOP}_{4}
theories, nor does it follow from co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
.

Example 4.2
.

Let
T
be the model companion of the theory of triangle-free tripartite graphs, with the partition denoted by
P_{1}(x),P_{2}(x),P_{3}(x)
as in Example
3.8
. Recall that
T
is
\mathrm{NSOP}_{4}
, and
T
is a free amalgamation theory in the sense of
[
11
]
, so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}b
if and only of
a\cap b\subseteq M
; see Proposition 4.3 of
[
36
]
. Let
p_{i}(x)=:P_{i}(x)
for
i=1,2,3
. Then the
p_{i}(x)
are internally stable–the structures
\mathcal{M}_{p_{i}}
have quantifier elimination in the unary language of
M
-definable subsets of
P_{i}(x)
. Internally stable types are always co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
: by the proof of Theorem
3.7
, if an internally stable type
p
is not co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, then in the theory of
\mathcal{M}_{p}
, there is a hypergraph sequence
\{R_{n}\}
, a model
\mathcal{M}
, and Morley sequences
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
and
\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
with
b_{0}\equiv_{\mathcal{M}}b^{\prime}_{0}
so that
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
is a clique and
\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
is an anti-clique. But this is impossible if the theory of
\mathcal{M}_{p}
is stable, as
b_{0}\equiv_{\mathcal{M}}b^{\prime}_{0}
implies
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}\equiv_{M}\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
when
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
and
\{b^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
are Morley sequences in a stable theory.

However, the conclusion of theorem
4.1
does not hold. Let
a_{1}\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq p_{1}(\mathbb{M})
,
a_{2}\models p_{2}(\mathbb{M})
,
a_{3}\models p_{3}(\mathbb{M})
with
\models a_{1}Ea_{2}
,
\models a^{\prime}_{1}Ea_{3}
,
\models a_{2}Ea_{3}
. Then
a_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{2}
,
a^{\prime}_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{3}
,
a_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a_{3}
, but
\mathrm{tp}(a_{1}/Ma_{2})\cup\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}_{1}/Ma_{3})
is inconsistent.

We prove theorem
4.1
, beginning with some observations on co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types. First of all, Conant-independence between co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types is just Kim-dividing independence.

Claim 4.3
.

If
b\subset p(\mathbb{M})
for
p(x)
a co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
type over
M
, then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
if and only if
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{Kd}_{M}b
.

Proof.

If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
, then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{Kd}_{M}b
by Kim’s lemma, Definition 3.1.3. Conversely, if
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{Kd}_{M}b
, then in particular, by compactness we can choose an
M
-finitely satisfiable Morley sequence
\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
,
b_{0}=b
, that is indiscernible over
Ma
. But then, by Fact
5.7
below, formulas that do not Kim-divide over
M
by a some
M
-finitely satisfiable Morley sequence do not Conant-fork over
M
, so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
.
∎

Claim 4.4
.

If
p(x),q(x)
are co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types over
M
and
a\subseteq p(\mathbb{M})
,
b\subseteq q(\mathbb{M})
, then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
if and only if
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}a
.

This follows from Proposition 3.1.

Claim 4.5
.

If
b\subset p(\mathbb{M})
for
p(x)
a co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
type over
M
, and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
, then for any
M
-finitely satisfiable type
q(x)
, there is a Morley sequence
I=\{b_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
in
q(x)
,
b_{0}=b
, that is indiscernible over
Ma
, and any such Morley sequence will satisfy
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I
.

This is the “chain condition”, Claim
3.4
, together with compactness. We use Claims
4.3
-
4.5
throughout.

Third, we have the weak independence theorem between
two
co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types, analogously to Proposition 6.1 of Kaplan and Ramsey,
[
19
]
:

Claim 4.6
.

Let
p(x),q(x)
be co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types over
M
, and let
a\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M})
,
b,c\subseteq q(\mathbb{M})
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
,
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c
,
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{u}_{M}b
. Then there is
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}bc
with
a^{\prime\prime}\models\mathrm{tp}(a/Mb)\cup\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/Mc)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Proposition 6.1 of
[
19
]
. By Claims
4.4
and
4.5
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
, let
I=\{a_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
be an
M
-invariant Morley sequence with
a_{0}=a
that is indiscernible over
Mb
. Again by Claims
4.4
and
4.5
.,
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c
and
a^{\prime}\equiv_{M}a
, for
r(x,y)=\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime},c)
,
\cup_{i<\omega}q(a_{i},y)
is consistent, so we can choose some
c^{\prime}\models\cup_{i<\omega}r(a_{i},y)
. By Ramsey’s theorem, compactness and an automorphism, we can choose
c^{\prime}
in particular so that
I
remains indiscernible over
Mbc^{\prime}
. So
bc^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}a
, and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}bc^{\prime}
, with
c^{\prime}a\equiv_{M}ca^{\prime}
.

Let
s(y)
be an
M
-finitely satisfiable type extending
\mathrm{tp}(c/Mb)
, and let
c^{\prime\prime}\models s(y)|_{Mbc^{\prime}}
, so
c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{Mb}c
and
c^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{u}bc^{\prime}
. As
c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M}c
, choose
b^{\prime}
with
b^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M}bc^{\prime}
; by left extension for
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{u}
,
b^{\prime}
can further be chosen with
b^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{u}bc^{\prime}
. Then
bc^{\prime},b^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}
begin an
M
-invariant Morley sequence
J
. As
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}bc^{\prime}
, there is an
M
-invariant Morley sequence
J^{\prime}\equiv_{bc^{\prime}}J
indiscernible over
Ma
; using claim
4.5
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}J^{\prime}
. Write
J^{\prime}=(bc^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime\prime},\ldots)
. Then
c^{\prime\prime\prime}a\equiv_{M}c^{\prime}a\equiv_{M}ca^{\prime}
,
c^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv_{Mb}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{Mb}c
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}bc^{\prime\prime\prime}
. By an
Mb
-automorphism taking
c^{\prime\prime}
to
c
, we obtain
a^{\prime}
as desired.

∎

Using this weak independence theorem, we can now show the full independence theorem between
two
co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types.

Claim 4.7
.

Let
p(x),q(x)
be co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types over
M
, and let
a\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}\subseteq p(\mathbb{M})
,
b,c\subseteq q(\mathbb{M})
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
,
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c
,
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
. Then there is
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}bc
with
a^{\prime\prime}\models\mathrm{tp}(a/Mb)\cup\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/Mc)
.

Proof.

We could have followed the proof of Theorem 6.5 of
[
19
]
, but we offer our own exposition. We first show that it suffices to show consistency of
\mathrm{tp}(a/Mb)\cup\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/Mc)
in the statement of Claim
4.9
. Let
r(x)
be an
M
-finitely satisfiable type extending
\mathrm{tp}(A/M)
. By Claims
4.4
and
4.5
, we can find
I=\{a_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
with
a_{0}=a
indiscernible over
Mb
and
I^{\prime}=\{a^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
with
a^{\prime}_{0}=a^{\prime}
indiscernible over
Mb
, both
M
-finitely satisfiable Morley sequences in
r(x)
. Then
I\equiv_{M}I^{\prime}
. So using the consistency, we can find
I^{\prime\prime}\models(I/Mb)\cup\mathrm{tp}(I^{\prime}/Mc)
, which by Ramsey’s theorem and compactness, can be assumed indiscernible over
Mbc
. So
I^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}bc
by claim
4.4
, and we can find
a^{\prime\prime}
in this sequence as desired.

Therefore, suppose
a,a^{\prime},b,c
are as in the statement of the claim, and
\mathrm{tp}(a/Mb)\cup\mathrm{tp}(b/Mc)
is inconsistent. By compactness, there are some
\varphi(x,b)\in\mathrm{tp}(a/Mb)
and
\psi(x,c)\in\mathrm{tp}(a/Mc)
with
\varphi(x,b)\cup\psi(x,c)
inconsistent. Let
s(y,z)=\mathrm{tp}(bc/M)
. We find
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\ldots\subset q(\mathbb{M})
,
c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}\subset q(\mathbb{M})
with the following properties

(1) For
m<n
,
b_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{i-1}\ldots b_{1}
for
i\leq m
, and
c_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}c_{i-1}\ldots c_{m+1}b_{m}\ldots b_{1}
for
m\leq i\neq n
. Thus by repeated applications of Claim
4.9
,
\{\varphi(x,b_{1}),\ldots\varphi(x,b_{m}),\psi(x,c_{m+1}),\ldots,\psi(x,c_{n})\}
is consistent.

(2) For
i<j
,
b_{i}c_{j}\models s(y,z)
, so
\varphi(x,b_{i})\psi(y,c_{j})
is inconsistent.

(3)
b_{1}\ldots,b_{n}\ldots\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K^{*}}c_{1}\ldots,c_{n}\ldots
.

By Fact
2.4
, this will give us a failure of co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
, a contradiction. We use the technique of Conant,
[
11
]
(though it is not yet necessary to get
\mathrm{SOP}_{3}
; this technique is similar to the “zig-zag lemma,” Lemma 6.4, from the original proof of the independence theorem in
[
19
]
). Assume
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n},c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}
already constructed, satisfying these properties up to
n
(including
c_{n}\equiv_{M}c
). By repeated instances of Claim
4.9
(applied to
q(x),q(x)
), and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}c
, there is
b^{\prime}_{n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}c_{1}\ldots c_{n}
with
b^{\prime}_{n+1}\models\cup_{i=1}^{n}s(y,c_{i})
. Again by Claim
4.9
,
b_{n}\ldots b_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c_{1},\ldots c_{n}
, and an automorphism, we can additionally choose
b^{\prime}_{n+1}=b_{n+1}
so that
b_{n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{1}\ldots b_{n}
and
b_{n+1}\ldots b_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c_{1},\ldots c_{n}
. Now choose
c_{n+1}\equiv_{M}c
with
c_{n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{u}c_{1}\ldots c_{n}b_{1}\ldots b_{n+1}
. We get
b_{n+1}\ldots b_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}c_{1},\ldots c_{n+1}
, by the proof of Claim
5.13
.
∎

. Call

Subclaim 4.10
.

M
, if
e\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}f
and
\mathrm{tp}(g/Mef)
extends to an
M
-invariant type
q(x)
, then
e\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}fg
. So by two applications of this fact and symmetry (claim
4.4
),
B_{0}\ldots B_{n}B_{n+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b^{\prime}_{0}\ldots b^{\prime}_{n}b^{\prime}_{n+1}
. This completes the induction, from which it follows that
I_{B}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
.
∎

Let
b^{\prime\prime}
be as in the subclaim. As
A\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}b
, for
p(X,y)=\mathrm{tp}(Ab/M)
, by claim
4.5
and an automorphism there is
A^{\prime}\models\cup_{i<\omega}p(X,b^{\prime\prime}_{i})
with
A^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
. By claims
4.4
and
4.5
, we can then find
\{A^{\prime}_{i}\}=I_{A^{\prime}}\equiv_{M}I_{A}
indiscernible over
MI_{b^{\prime\prime}}
with
A^{\prime}_{0}=A^{\prime}
and
I_{A^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
. So we have
I_{A^{\prime}}\equiv_{M}I_{A}
and
I_{A^{\prime}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
,
I_{A}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{B}
by indiscernibility of
I_{B}
over
I_{A}
and claim
4.5
, and
I_{B}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K^{*}}_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
by the subclaim. So by the independence theorem between two co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types (Claim
4.7
) and an automorphism, there is some
\{b^{*}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}=I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}
with
I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}\equiv_{MI_{B}}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}
and
I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}I_{A}\equiv_{M}I_{b^{\prime\prime}}I_{A^{\prime}}
. The sequence
\{b^{*}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}=I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}
will have the following three properties:
b^{*}_{i}\models p(A_{j},y)
, so
b^{*}_{i}\equiv_{MA_{j}}b
, for
i,j<\omega
,
\{B_{i}b^{*}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}
form an
M
-invariant Morley sequence, and
b^{*}_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}B_{i}
for
i<\omega
. If we extract mutually indiscernible sequences from
I_{A},I_{B}I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}},I_{C}
, finding
\check{I}_{A},\check{I}_{B}\check{I}^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}},\check{I}_{C}
as in Fact
4.8
, then
I_{A}I_{B}I_{C}\equiv_{M}\check{I}_{A}\check{I}_{B}\check{I}_{C}
, so we may assume
\check{I}_{A}=I_{A}
,
\check{I}_{B}=I_{B}
,
\check{I}_{C}=I_{C}
and then
\check{I}^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}=\{\check{b}_{i}^{*}\}_{i<\omega}
will also have these three properties that
\{b^{*}_{i}\}_{i<\omega}=I^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}}
has. Let
b^{\prime}=\check{b}_{0}^{*}
. Then
b^{\prime}\equiv_{MA}b
,
b^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}B
and
I_{A},I_{B}\check{I}^{*}_{b^{\prime\prime}},I_{C}
will be mutually indiscernible
M
-invariant Morley sequences, so
A,Bb^{\prime},C
form a generic triple.
∎

Now we find
a^{1},\ldots,a^{n},\ldots\models\mathrm{tp}(a_{1}/M)
,
b^{1},\ldots,b^{n},\ldots\models\mathrm{tp}(a_{2}/M)
, and
c^{1},\ldots,c^{n},\ldots\models\mathrm{tp}(a_{3}/M)
with the following properties:

(1) For
i<j
,
a^{j}c^{i}\models q_{2}(x,z)
,
a^{i}b^{j}\models q_{3}(x,y)
,
b^{i}c^{j}\models q_{1}(y,z)
.

(2) For
i<\omega
,
a^{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}a^{1}\ldots a^{i-1}
,
b^{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}b^{1}\ldots b^{i-1}
, and
c^{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}c^{1}\ldots c^{i-1}

(3) For each
n<\omega

a^{1}\ldots a^{n}
,
b^{1}\ldots b^{n}
,
c^{1}\ldots c^{n}
form a generic triple.

Assume
a^{1},\ldots,a^{n}
,
b^{1},\ldots,b^{n}
,
c^{1},\ldots,c^{n}
already constructed, satisfying these properties up to
n
. As for
i\leq n
,
a^{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}a^{1}\ldots a^{i-1}
, we can find some
b\models\cup^{n}_{i=1}q_{3}(a^{i},y)
with
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}a^{1}\ldots a^{n}
, by, say, repeated applications of the independence theorem between two co-
\mathrm{NSOP}_{1}
types, Claim
4.7
(though could have stated the claim so that we need less than this). Then letting
a^{1},\ldots,a^{n}=A
,
b^{1},\ldots,b^{n}=B
,
c^{1},\ldots,c^{n}=C
, we can choose
b_{n+1}=b^{\prime}
as in Claim
4.9
, while will be as desired. Symmetrically, we find
c_{n+1}
and
a_{n+1}
.

Now let

\Phi(x^{1},y^{1},z^{1};x^{2},y^{2},z^{2})=q_{2}(x^{2},z^{1})\cup q_{3}(x^{1},y^{2})\cup q_{1}(y^{1},z^{2})

By (1) this has infinite chains, so by
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
and compactness it has a
3
-cycle: some

(d^{1},e^{1},f^{1},d^{2},e^{2},f^{2},d^{3},e^{3},f^{3})

\models\Phi(x^{1},y^{1},z^{1};x^{2},y^{2},z^{2})\cup\Phi(x^{2},y^{2},z^{2};x^{3},y^{3},z^{3})\cup\Phi(x^{3},y^{3},z^{3};x^{1},y^{1},z^{1})

In particular,
(d^{1},e^{2},f^{3})\models q_{3}(x,y)\cup q_{1}(y,z)\cup q_{2}(x,z)
, as desired. This concludes the proof of Theorem 4.2.

5.
\mathrm{NSOP}_{3}
theories with symmetric Conant-independence

In
[
40
]
, Simon proves the following independence theorem for
\mathrm{NTP}_{2}
theories, using the independence theorem for
\mathrm{NTP}_{2}
theories of Ben Yaacov and Chernikov (
[
42
]
).

Fact 5.1
.

Let
T
be
\mathrm{NTP}_{2}
, and let
